if it consists of more than one volume.

7. The borrower shall be responsible
for the value of the books lent out to him if
they are lost, damaged or otherwise cannot
be returned to the Library when they are
due or recalled.

parts wiz. Astronomy and Astrology, are so
connected that a knowledge of both of them is
absolutely essential as on the one hand predictions
are impossible to be made without knowing the.
course, position, etc., of each and every planet by -
the aid of the calculative portion, while on the
other hand, the knowledge of the planetary course,
position etc., will not by itself imbue a know-
ledge of their joint or several effects of which the
predictive portion treats. There are several works
on each of these two branches of the science and one ~
possessed of a knowledge of both of them is known
as an astrologer [b] (=afaa). The six subdivisions ~—

has to find out what the joint and several effects of
the planets are on a particular person at a parti-
cular time considered from their course, position, —

The original authors of the science are said to
be the following eighteen wz., (1) Surya (2) Brah-
man (3) Vyasa (4) Vasishta (5) Athri (6) Para-
sara (7): Kasyapa (8) Narada (9) *Glarga: (10),
Marichi (11) Manu (12) Angiras (13) Lomasa
(14) Paulisa (15) Chyavana (16) Yavana (17)
Manu [1] and (18) Sounaka. [c]

But a complete and reliable version of the
work of any one of them has not yet been discovered;
only certain portions of their works or a few
slokas thereof being met with here and there and
most of these have been published having been
quoted in some place or other.

Greeks. But the [Indian sages not only seem to have
paid no regard to the fact that the Yavanas were
foreigners and to have freely accepted them to their
society but have gone even so far as to style them
as Iswaras (Lords’ on account of their immense
knowledge of the science, neither of which the
present Hindus would dare do. This would go
to show that caste system did not then exist or
that, if it did exist, knowledge was considered ~
superior to caste prejudice and could transcend
all such “imaginary objections and occupy the
highest place. Varahamihira, the greatest astro-
loger of the later ages whose esteemed work, the
Brihatjataka, is the daily and inseparable com-
panion of every astrologer of the present day,
speaks of the Yavanas in very high terms. [d|

Having said that a complete work of any one
of the above-mentioned 18 authors is not available,
it need hardly be pointed out that the periods of
their existence are also enveloped in profound
obscurity. It is believed that they lived some time
between the creation of the world and some cen-
turies before Christ.

Later astrologers among whom were‘1) Vishnu
Gupta better known as Chanakya of Chandragupta’s
pericd) (2) Devaswami (3) Siddhasena (4) Maya
(5) Manittha, (6) Satyacharya and others though
not sages were very great and learned scholars

who studied and understood the. works of the
original authors and wrote shorter works of
their own. The works of these astrologers have
also suffered the same fate as those of their pre-
decessors and a complete work of any one of these
is not available. The period of existence of these
great astrologers is also unknown and unascertain-
able except that they lived some time before the
great Varahamihira, one of the nine seins of the
court of King Vikramaditya. <i

Since Varahamihira, several astrologers came
into existence and some of them are authors of
certain astrological works. One of these is Kal-
yanavarman, the author of Saravali and our congra-
tulations are due to the learned translator of the
Jatakaparijata for having brought this very rare
work within the reach of the public. Then came
the later works such as Horasara, Jatakabharana,
Horaratna, Phaladeepixa Sarvartha Chintamani,
Jatakaparijata, Prasnaratnam, Prasnasanzraham,
Shatpanchasika, Vidvajenana Vallapbha, Gnanpra-
dipika, Anushtanapadhati, Krishneeyam, Prasna-
prakarana, Charasangraha Muhurtaratnam, Sarva-
siddhi, Muhurta Abharanam Muhurtasangraham,
Acharasangraham, Kalapadhati, Sripatipadhati,
Mandaviya, etc. These works are neither elaborate
nor wholly original. Some of them are more or
less an abridgment of the earlier works where-
from several slokas are transcribed verbatim or
with slight alterations. One of such works is the
Jatakaparijata wherein about 75 per cent of the
slokas are transcribed from earlier works and

6 INTRODUCTION.

the rest are supposed. to be the authors own.
The author Vaidyanatha Dikshita appears to have
been a talented and very highly educated man
of his day and he has not failed to hit upon
the best and choicest slokas for his selections,
which are from Gargasamhita, Brihat Jataka,
Saravali. Parasarahora, Jatakaratna, Mandaviya
Jataka and such other works of classical authors.

The author of the Jatakaparijata has confined
his attention to horoscopy, an important branch of
the predictive portion of the science, but notwith-
standing the smallness of the volume,. has brought
in the choicest materials to help the student in
predicting correctly and has thus made his work
superior to some of the other works of later astro-
logers.

Prediction, according to the science, is neither
a random guess-work nor fortune telling which
certain street astrologers and professional mendi-
cants have taken up as a pretentive means of
honest living. Scientific predictions are neither as
easy as several seem to suppose, nor is it a perform-
ance that can be done in a few minutes. The
study of the science will itself take a number of
years and reading a horoscope and predicting the
past, present and future of the owner thereof
scientifically will ordinarily occupy a month or
more and it is seldom or never that an honest
astrologer gets himself adequately remunerated for
his work. Honesty had therefore to be shoved into
the pigeon hole and predictions had the fate of ran-

dom shots and it is no wonder that the science began
to be looked upen with suspicion and distrust by
persons whose want of a knowledge of it prevented
them from weighing the merits or demerits of
those astrologers. The foreign invasions of India,
the downfall of Sanskrit education and .want of
facilities and encouragement and consequent
neglect and indifference, all have woefully told

upon all the valuable works on this science and it

is now impossible to believe that any of the few

works which are at present within the reach of

the public is free from interpolations and errors.

Some of the later generations had even become so

unscrupulous that they did not hesitate to score

out the original author's name and insert their

own or their friend’s. An example of this may be

seen from the following sloka.[e] of Vikramaditya

the fourth tz of which would appear to have been

spuriously altered into a different reading by some

of Magha’s patrons and well-wishers. The fourth

(aq) of the sloka given is also found in another’
form [f] which is inconsistent with tradition and

common sense.

Recent scientific and astronomical researches
are however giving some fair nursing to this branch
of the almost decayed science. It is still in its
weak state and requires careful handling and pro-
per nourishment before it can seca, attain to the

same glorious state it once occupied. This care
and attention should be bestowed on it by the
whole universe; and the Indians should exert all the
more towards its improvement as it is towards the
revival of a science which their ancestors acquired
after great trouble and severe penance for a series of
years and handed down to the succeeding genera-
tions for their guidance. Most of the readers may
be aware that Chyavana, one of the above-named
18 authors of the science, had to perform such
severe penance in regard to certain observations in
connection with this science that, by the time he
succeeded in his attempt, he was attacked with
consumption and had to get the aid of the celestial
doctors the Asvini Devas) to cure him. The medicine
used for the cure of this great sage’s disease
is still known as Chyavana Prasa saaagiat and is
one of the most valuable and effective medicines
the Indian Pharmacopia is proud of. It therefore
needs no repetition that the difficulties encountered
by each and every one of the original authors
(sages) should have been enormous and every effort
should be made to revive, preserve and, if possible,
augment the result of such laborious works of so
many great sages.

One way of doing this is by translating the
available works into languages most commonly
used inthe present day. Sanskrit, in which the
original works are written has undergone the same
fate as this branch of the scienc2 itself and there
are but a few scholars thoroughly conversant with
the language. A good translation into English—

INTRODUCTION. 9

the world’s present day language—is the only means
of making the works readable, intelligible and useful
to the public and such of the Sanskrit scholars
as will devote a few of their valuable hours in
translating the original Sanskrit works will not only
help themselves but help the public. If they
should be Indians, they will also be doing good to
their mother country.

Leaving the origin of the science and the mys-
terious and unavailable state of the original works,
it is now necessary to turn to the primary princi-
ples of the science itself and the scope of the work
under consideration.

The Indian sages, as already suggested, dis-
covered the existence of natural forces, such as
eravitation, attraction, repulsion, etc, long before
any other nation and attributed these forces as
originating from the position, etc., of the seven
planets, vz., the Sun, the Moon, Mars, Mercury,
Jupiter, Venus and Saturn. The two other planets
Rahu and Ketu, as also several other subsidiary
_ planets as Mandi, etc., were also discovered to exist,
but their influence on the universe depended more
on the owner of the house they occupied and they
had no houses of their own. We shall therefore
only deal with the seven planets first mentioned.
Of those seven planets, the Sun stands foremost
and the Moon comes next. For Iswara (God) said
to Parvati:

“fam of the nature of the Sun and Thou art
declared to be the Moon. The whole universe

10 INTRODUCTION.

consisting of mobile and immobile objects is
brought about by our attraction and repulsion.” [g]

The zodiac is divided into twelve signs or Rasis
which are called (1) Mesha (Aries), (2) Vrishabha
(Taurus), (3) Mithuna (Gemini), (4) Kataka (Can-
cer), (5) Simha (Leo), (6) Kanya (Virgo), (7) Tula
(Libra), (8) Vrischika (Scorpio), (9) Dhanus (Sagi-
tarius), (10' Makara (Capricornus), (11) Kumbha
(Aquarius) and (12) Meena (Pisces)... ,

~ The Sun and the Moon are said to have once
owned all these twelve signs between themselves,
six signs from Cancer upwards by the Moon and
six signs from Leo downwards by the Sun. [bh]

The Sun and the Moon are kings, Mars is
commander, Mercury is prince, Jupiter and Venus
are ministers and Saturn is servant. [i] The Sun and
the Moon gave each of the remaining five planets
in recognition of their valuable services a house in
turn. The Moon gave Mithuna, Vrishabha, Mesha,
Meena and Kumbha respectively to Mercury,
Venus, Mars, Jupiter and Saturn. while the Sun
gave them each Kanya, Tula, Vrischika, Dhanus
and Makara in the same order. Thus the Sun and
the Moon became possessed of only one house each

INTRODUCTION. 11

while the remaining five planets became possessed
of two houses each.

Though the Sun and the Moon are, as stated
above, all-powerful and the originators of the
several forces governing the universe, the remaining
five planets also possess certain powers and influ-
ence over the universe, and everything thereon.
These planets further perform certain special func-
tions. The Sun has greater influence on the soul,
the Moon on the mind, Mars on physical strength, —
Mercury on learning and eloquence, Jupiter on
happiness and wisdom, Venus on worldly pleasures
and Saturn on miseries. And when consulting the
horoscope of a person, his position in life, learning,
etc., have to be concluded from the position, etc.,
of the planets. There are several other general
and special functions appertaining to each of these
planets, but itis not necessary to enter into all
those details in an introduction. Our object here
is to tell the readers that the Indian sages had
discovered that natural forces which govern the
_ universe emanated from the planets and that they
have their influence on all animate and inanimate
objects, atmospherical and other changes on the ~
face of the globe.

The twelve houses are, for the purposes of
predictions on a horoscope, etc., usually counted
from the Lagna which is also called the Rising Sign
or .Birth Sign, and-is known as the first house and
the succeeding ones as second, third, fourth, fifth
and so on up- to the twelfth and they represent:

12 INTRODUCTION..

(1) Body, (2) Wealth, (3) Brothers, (4) Friends,
(0) Sons, (6) Enemies, (7) Wife, (8) Death, (9) Happi-
ness, (10) Profession, (11) Income and {12) Ex-
penditure, They are then styled Bhavas.

In the Sanskrit language, as readers may be
aware, there are more than one word to name an
object or to represent an idea. So these twelve
signs (bhavas', their respective position as to each

other, the planets, etc., are found to be called by~

more than one name and some of these such as
Kendra, Panapara, Apoklima, Dyuna, Ripha, etc.,
are alleged to be of Greek origin. The simi-
larities and coincidences between the Greek and
Hindu systems of Astrology are so striking and
the admission by the ancient sages as well as
by Varahamihira of the Yavanas (Greeks) as
authorities in this science is so specific that the
introduction of the Greek terms, if they be so, is
more than accidental or unintentional. We see
the works of the Yavanas, though in piecemeal,
written in such flowing style and pleasant metre
that it would begin vain to doubt their mastery
over the Sanskrit language. It might therefore be
possible that the Indian sages accepted several of
their terms which by usage got sanskritized and
found easy admission into the works on the science.
Or it might be that they are technical terms used
by the earliest Indian sages. The Chaldeans and
Egyptians are admitted to have interchanged their
views and opinions with the Indians from times
much éarlier than those that are ascribed to the
visits of the Yavanas (Greeks) to India.

ad

INTRODUCTION. 13

Kalidasa, who for the materials of his work
Raghuvamsa admits as having relied on the works
of earlier authors [j], speaks of the existence of the
Yavanas (Yavana women) at the time of Raghu [k].
This can lead us to no other conclusion than that a
class of foreigners known by the name of Yavanas,
(be they Greeks, Egyptians or Chaldeans) were in
India at the time of Raghu or perhaps earlier and
the fact of their women’s presence as well will
show that they were more than casual visitors.
They were probably temporary settlers in India at
the time. Again the word (afétt) Madira which
means ‘spirituous liquor’ or more commonly wine,
deserves consideration as throwing some light on
the remoteness of the visits of the Western nation
(Yavanas) to India. The word has perhaps its
origin from the Medeira Islands off Spain and
North Africa or the Island got itself so named on
account of the delicious wine it exported to India
from times immemorial. The fact that the Yava-
nas are mentioned as one of the eighteen original
authors of the science should also be remembered.

Then again, Alexander the Great. when he
invaded Babylon about the last quarter of the 4th
Century B.C., found that the Chaldeans had preserv-
ed the discoveries of the first astronomers in the
world with the names by which we still know the
stars and that he accepted copies of the registers

of their observations which ranged back for 1903
years from then as a welcome treasure and des-

take us back to about 42 centuries from now and
it is but possible that the registers, copies of which
were sent by Alexander to Aristotle, began to be
written up and maintained after the discoveries had
been systematized. There is therefore nothing in--
consistent or unreasonable in assigning to the first
astronomers or discoverers a much earlier period
which, if acceptable, would carry us to the periods
when Parasara and some other Indian sages and
astronomers are reputed to have lived.

It might also bs that these terms were fixed
upon by the Indians in consultation with the ©
Chaldeans and Egyptians who in turn gave them to
their students, the Greeks. The Greeks are sup-
posed not to have had any systematic knowledge
of Astronomy or Astrology till about the 5th cen-
tury after Uhrist. Astronomy and Astrology were
at that time ina very highly advanced state in
India and required no borrowing. So, the borrow-
ing, if any, cannot have been from the Greeks if the
word “Yavanas” meant “Greeks” and Greeks alone,
but from their masters, the Chaldeans, which again
is yet an unsettled fact.

It is therefore not unlikely that the Yavanas
who were visiting India very frequently or who had
almost settled down in India augmented their
original knowledge of the science by studying the
principles, etc., as laid down by the Indian sages,

and this should account for the superiority of their
knowledge as stated above. It does not follow
from this that the Greeks were the masters of the
Indians, nor that the science or any part thereof
was borrowed by the Indians from them. Jyotisha
(saa) to the Indians has been like ministers to
the king. For anything an Indian may want to do
he would see tothe time being good or bad and the
undertaking being auspicious and successful or
otherwise. Much more so with regard to the
ceremonies. Vedanga Jyostisha says that sacrifi-
ces are enjoined to be performed according to time
and he who knows how to sacrifice knows stra
(Astronomy and Astrology) [1]. Sacrifices were en-
joined by the Vedas which are supposed to have been
arranged by Vyasa who is said to have lived more
than 5000 years ago. Vyasa was the son of Para-
sara, remnants of whose works we still possess. We
also possess remnants of the works of Gargi, prob-
ably a still older sage than Parasara, being the son
of Brahma, We have again remnants of the work
of Bhrigu who had the capacity of deciding that
Vishnu alone deserved to be worshipped. Further
more,J yotisha forms one of the six auxiliaries of the
Vedas and is said to be the head ornament of the
Vedas [m] or their very eyes [n] (as valuable as the

eyes). These should more than establish that the
origin of Jyotisha in India was simultaneous with
the origin of the Vedas. These Vedas are supposed
to have been revealed by the Supreme Being and are
not human compositions They are therefore called
stfa (what is heard or revealed) as opposed to safe
(what is remembered or is the work of human
origin). The discussions of these serious questions
as to (1) who the Yavanas were, (2) when they
came to India, and (3) what and how much, if any |
of the science was taken from and given to either
the Greeks or any one else by the Indians, should,
I think, be taken up and decided by greater and
abler scholars and I feel that my knowledge and
authorities are but scanty to enter into a discus-
sion of such larger questions as these.

David Eugene Smith, Professor of Mathematics
in the Teachers’ College, Columbia University, New
York, in his introduction to the Ganita Sara Sangra-
ha translated by Professor Rangachari, says: “As to
“the relation between the East and West, we should
‘now bein a position tosay rather definitely that
“there is no evidence of any considerable influence
“of Greek algebra upon that of India. The two
“subjects are radicallydilferan in...) ras patente ce The
“Hindu works have a style and a range of topics
‘“neculiarly their own. Their problems lack the cold
“clear geometric precision of the West; they are
“clothed in that poetic language which distin-
“euishes the East and they relate to subjects that
“find no place in the scientific books of the

Greeks.”

INTRODUCTION. 17

From what has been said above, the chances

of escaping from the following probable but tradi-
tional and self-evident conclusions are few and far
between even though “appeal to common sense and
reason” is the only asylum of resort in the absence
of documentary evidence to support and substanti-
ate the conclusions

1.

That the original discoverers and authors of the
science were none other than the Indians.

That the Indian sages alone first discovered

(a) the existence of the planets and named them,
(b) the natural forces such as gravitation, attraction,
repulsion, etc.,

(c) that these forces emanated from the planets, and

(d) that the planets by their powers and influence
governed the whole universe and everything
thereon.

That the Chaldeans had had interchange of views and
opinions with the Indian sages from very early times.

That the visit to India of the Yavanas must have
been much earlier than what is ascribed to them now
unless Kalidasa is ruthlessly proposed to be charged
with imprudence in bringing about a historical in-
accuracy by the introduction of foreign matters that
did not find a place in the works of earlier authors
and that in writing the history ofa line of solar kings
whom he highly respected and revered ; and

That the Indians, Chaldeans and the Yavanas worked —
hand in hand inthe subsequent researches and im-
provement of the science.

The birth place therefore of the Jyotisha Sastra
is, | may safely conclude, India and that it is the
science which exhibits the light by which it is

possible to find and decide the past, present and the
future of all universal occurrences and that branch
thereof known as hososcopy bestows on man that
power by the aid of which he is enabled to read the
destinies [o] of the owner of the horoscope. The
twelve houses, their lords, occupiers, aspectors, etc.,
represent the destiny which paves the ways for the
sense to adopt. [p|

A knowledge of the effects due to the house
the bhava represents, its lord, occupant, etc., is
the light by which destiny is read.

Jatakaparijata, which, as already said, consists
of the choicest selections from earlier works of
great sages and eminent astrologers (and of which
as the author himself says, the matter has been
culled from every astrological work) is, I have no_
hesitation to say, the best available compendium
on horoscopy embcdying therein several useful
but rare informations not contained in any other
work of its kind. I do not mean by this that

INTRODUCTION. 19

a study of the Jatakaparijata will of itself make the
‘student proficient in the science. The science is,
as Varahamihira has said. a vast ocean difficult
to be crossed by men of ordinary talents and an
abridged work cannot of itself convey the full
extent of requisite knowledge necessary for detailed
predictions. Several other elaborate works will
have to be read before one can become a scholar of
the science, but the contents of the Jatakaparijata
will, unlike similar other works, more than initiate |
him into the heart of the science and be helpful to
him to widen his knowledge by a study of larger
works but to be practised by him at all times.

Little is known about Vaidyanatha Dikshita,
the author of the Jatakaparijata, except from what
he has himself said in the 2nd and last slokas of the
work, that he is a descendant of the Bharadwaja
family and son of Venkatadri. Some say that he
is the identical Vaidyanatha, the author of Prata-
parudra, and some other works and annotations. If
this be so, he must have lived about the 13th
century A.D. The word Sri.(sit) that-is prefixed to his
name would lead to the conclusion that this is the
same Vaidyanatha who is alse the author of the
other works above stated and if a different parent-
-ageis brought ‘in, itis only the spurious work of
some unscrupulous hands. _

In the colophon [q! of another work,the author
has used his name just in the same way as he has
[q] 2a fret gett iter afearPayat wae |
faseitaaanaa a Gara |

20 INTRODUCTION.

used it in the second and the last slokas of Jataka-
parijata. But as Mr. Shankar P. Pandit mM. A. has-
said “one source not yet much investigated, of
‘facts bearing upon the ages of Sanskrit authors is
“the numerous commentaries on Kavyas, grammars
“and lastly the works on the Alankara Sastra.
“They are full of quotations and the date of one
“being ascertained will throw considerable light on
“those of many others and make the subject of
“Indian chronology, which has hitherto remained
‘‘ shrouded in obscurity and confusion, afford results
“far beyond our expectations.” I therefore refrain
from making any statement: about the author except
that his work, the Jatakaparijata, shows that he is
a highly learned and talented man.

The translation into English of most of the
Oriental languages is a very difficult task. Much
more so itis when Sanskrit happens to be the
language that has to be translated from. In trans-
lating, the original life and spirit is usually lost,
only the skeleton which is probably only the bare
substance or idea of the portion translated being
left behind. But our translator has so closely kept
to the text that he must be credited as having
successfully discharged the onerous work he under-
took to perform more for the good of the public and
with a patriotic spirit than for anything else.

RANGOON, \ N. PARAMESWARA IYER.
24th August 1915.

FOREWORD BY THE ANNOTATOR.

About adecade ago, I placed before the public
an English translation of the Jatakaparijata and
the encouragement accorded to it by the public
has induced me to publish these notes, which I
hope will meet with the same approbation as the
translation itself.

Several works of Astrology such as (IL) Pra-
- japatya (2) Brihatprajapatya (3) Surya Hora (4)
Skanda Hora (5) Vasishta Hora (6) Sounaka Hora
(7) Kousika Hora (8) Garga Hora (9) Yavana
Hora (10) Mandavya Jataka etc, are not wholly
available, though several slokas therefrom are met
with here and there. There are other works such
as (1) Jatakabharana (2) Sarvartha Chintamani
(3) Phaladeepika (4) Horamakaranda (5) Jata-
kadesa etc., but these are not complete in themsel-
- ves as they do not deal with all the "branches of
Horoscopy. In Jatakaparijata one will not fail
to find that the author Vaidyanatha Dikshita has
judiciously brought together exceptionally valuable
materials from several works of classical authors
and that he has touched upon almost all the
branches. Brihat Parasara Hora which is now
available though complete in itself, is too volumi-
nous. I therefore selected Jatakaparijata for my
translation.

22 FOREWORD BY THE ANNOTATOR.

{ have in these notes supplied and supple-
mented in appropriate places information regard-
ing Rasmi, Grahavasthas, Madhyamayuryoga,
Rajayoga, Ashtakavarza, Kalachakra and Dasas
which were either omitted or only briefly touched
upon or dealt with in the Jatakaparijata, Advant-
age has also been taken of this opportunity
to revise the original translation of some of the
slokas of the text and to furnish additional expla-
nations accompanied by illustrations where such
elucidations were considered necessary. The elasti-
city of the Sanskrit language is such that the same
passage often admits of several interpretations,
in some cases all the interpretations standing good,
while in others one particular interpretation alone
holding good with the result that the interpreter, in
the absence of parallel passages bearing on the
same point from other astrological wokrs, is at a
loss to decide what meaning or meanings were in-
tended by the author of the passages. No pains
have been spared to collect together and quote all
available parallel passages freely and fully, almost
perhaps to superfluity, so that the translation with
these notes may prove to be a self-contained and
useful compendium on the subject.

A few words about the object of the study of
Astrology may not be out of place here. It goes
without saying that every person aspires for world-
ly prosperity. It has been admitted by eminent
persons of all times that natural forces exert their
influences upon the’ human mind. The actions
of individuals in their every-day life depend upon

FOREWORD BY THE ANNOTATOR. 23

the frame of their mind on the particular occasion.
These natural forces emanate from the planets. It
may have been within the actual experience of
many that the modus operand: of any action
depends a great deal upon the foresight and wisdom
of the individual. Master minds have the capacity
of seeing through the good and bad influence at
work at the time and of understanding the nature
of their surroundings and of adjusting themselves
in such a way as to secure the endin view. But
such persons are very few. Humanity in general
is endowed only with ordinary intellect. Mighty
intellects of yore—those masterminds of the past—
have for the benefit of their posterity, given syste-
matically the effects capable of being produced by
the several positions etc., of the planets. To speak
of their stupendous work asthe product of idle
fancy is to betray ignorance. They have prescrib-
ed rules to be observed for embarking on important
undertakings by reference to the positions of the
planets, so that one may be certain that, if it is
begun at an auspicious time, it will be attended
_ with success. The forces exerted by the planets at
such a time will tend to produce benefic results
without any great effort on the part of the person
concerned. If otherwise the. desired effect will
_ hardly be achieved even at the cost of great efforts.

Planets have been declared to be productive of
malefic effects when in certain positions. It is
however an error to suppose that these results will
inexorably come about. Various remedies have
been recommended to mitigate, if not to ward off

24 FOREWORD BY THE ANNOTATAR.

these malefic influences by the performance of
Shantis such as Japa, Homa, Archana, etc., the
efficacy of these depending upon the efficiency of
their performance and the sincerity of the perform-
er. The study of Astrology will therefore bestow
a knowledge, by means of which one can ascertain
when he may have favourable or unfavourable times
and how he may alleviate the impending misfor-
tune if any. Herein lies the real value of
Astrology. In the case of certain horoscopes the
position of planets will be such that the natives
thereof will be able to develop a strong will
power. In these cases it will be found that they
can adapt themselves to various conditions and
act in such a way as to emerge unaffected by the
malefic influences, if any, resulting from the
inauspicious positions of planets at the time of
birth or at the commencement of any action as the
case may be. To be fore-warned is to be fore-armed.
A knowledge of Astrology equips one with such
arms and it only remains for one to use his will
power wherever possible, to shape his course of
action for averting the evil and securing the
good.

-T cannot close the preface without expressing
my deep sense of gratitude and acknowledgment
of the valuable help received from my late revered
father in the huge task of translating and publish-
ing the Jatakaparijata. But for his valuable
advice and co-operation in bringing out clearly the
meaning of several difficult passages, the work
would not have been satisfactorily accomplished.

FOREWORD BY THE ANNOTATOR. ee as

In bringing out these notes, my acknowledg-
ments are due to several of my friends, but in
particular to Mr. N. Parameswara lyer of Rangoon.
His lucid explanations of difficult passages,
valuable suggestions and copious quotations in
support of his views from works inaccessible to me
were a welcome treasure and stood me in good
stead. The considerable trouble and _ personal
inconvenience which this work necessarily entailed
on him bear ample testimony to his self-sacrificing
nature. In short, the value of these notes and
their publication even at this distant date, are to
a very large extent due to his timely help and
strenuous exertions.

I am fully aware of the fact that the whole
work as now published is capable of improve-
ment. I solicit the indulgence of the reader for
its deficiencies, and shall be grateful for any
suggestions for improvement which will be taken
advantage of when bringing out the next edition of
the work.

sumes in the ae an elementary knowledge of the science,
such as the names of the 12 Rasis, the 27 Nakshatras,
etc., and begins to give the different names by which each of
the signs is known.

dealt with; but the following from Skanda hora (S#FeERt)

Sloka 8. The 12 Rasis are supposed to represent the se-

mialefic planets.

In the case of all horoscopes, the several parts of the
body referred to above should be understood to be represented
by the signs beginning from the Lagna.

Sloka 11. Some books read aera.

Sloka 18. This sloka is helpful in deciding the sex, appear-
ance, character, etc. of the person concerned. If the Lagna
be a male sign and the surroundings lead to the same con-
clusion, he is surely a male; but if they should be otherwise,
he will be a male with feminine appearance and character,
etc., or vice versa. The student should never venture any
prediction unless all the existing conditions are fully and
carefully tested and weighed. cf. CATA:

BAT: FEA AAT AA TATA AT BeAr: |

be seen from sloka 56 infra that the sign’s length is represent-

short sign. Vide also notes to that sloka.

According to the Yavanas, the lords of the Horas and
Drekkanas are as described in the following sloka:
MUM F at Waa aegenrsaragafedtar |

so that, according to this view, the ownership of the Horas is’
not restricted to the Sun and the Moon, but is shared by al]
the planets. But this view of the Yavanas is not recognised
by Varahamihira, nor even by authorities like Satya who has
thus stated.

Sloka 31. This sloka gives the rule for finding out the lord of
the Saptamamsa in the cases of odd and even signs,

In the translation, for the words “and other houses” read “and

the planets.” The Vargas appertain to Lagna and the planets

only and not to houses.

739

Sloka 32-33. These give the rules for finding out the
Navamsas. Each Navamsa is 3°20’. Divide the Sphutam by
3°91! and the result will indicate the number of Navamsas the
Lagna or the planet in question has traversed.

Sloka 34. It will be seen that the Vargottama Navamsas
in the several Rasis bear the same names asthe signs them-
selves. For example, the first Navamsa of Mesha is Mesha;
the first of Kataka is Kataka and so on. The middlemost
Navamsain Vrishabha is Vrishabha & so on- cf.

eq gay ayes Y antaaea aaah: |

ductive of immense good-cf. FaATaR

Sloka 44. If planets be in their’ Moolatrikona, Swa-
kshetra, Uccha or Vargottama positions and have other benefic
portions arising from the Dasa Vargas, then, Uttama and
other Vaiseshikamsas referred to in the next two and a half

Slokas 58-64. According to some, the biped, quadruped and
the reptile signs become strong when they happen to bein
Kendra, Panaphara and Apoklima houses respectively. This
view is not accepted by NEVA, for he says:

HMA: Saar: TARA FAT: |
aaa: A Ceaarer TT: HAT: I

According to Balabhadra, the terms Kendra, Panaphara
etc. are applied only to Bhavas and not Rasis. For he says

“aaa Seance vrata a catteafata aeraq | arte
rat uit aerate zo aceete centaq Aesral
Tah: | aa a waftawa Heaneaata qeectastraged, eT
qfa gaat AAS Westeasaaeta LITT Il

In this chapter, the terms used to indicate one or the
other of the twelve houses or a number of them may
be divided into two classes, viz, (1) those which are used
as mere conventional names serving no other purpose than
that of denoting the particular house or houses they have been
specially elected to designate and (2) those which, besides
serving such purpose, also connote the functions of the
houses they signify.

Of the former class may be mentioned the following :—
Hora (aizt) for the Lagna; Duschikya (sf) for the 3rd
house ; Meshurana (HITT) for the 10th; Rippha (f2:m) for the
12th; Chaturasra (aqtet) for the 4th and 8th houses; Kan-
taka (Huta), Kendra (rq) and Chatushtaya (“@q¥ay) for the

739

Ist, 4th, 7th and 10th houses; Panaphara(qorgz) for the. 2nd,
5th, 8th and 11th houses; and Apoklima (gm@tfayv) for the
ord, 6th, 9th and 12th houses.

The following belong to the latter class. Tanu (ag) for
the Lagna, (meaning thereby that everything about the body—
its growth or decay—health, etc., has to be ascertained from
the Lagna; rq or | for the 2nd house, thus indicating
that information regarding one’s family, dayadins and wealth
has to be predicted from the 2nd house; @gq@ or faa for the
3rd house ; qed, asa or J@, Ga for the 4th house; eft or ate,
qa, etc., for the 5th house; and so on,

It will be seen that the figures given in the slokain the
text aggregate 360 and are the measurements in degrees of
the 12 signs which they subtend at the centre.
we As the whole zodiac is passed over in 24 hours, each
degree is finished in 24/360 x 60 x 60 or 240 seconds or 240/24 or
10 Vighatikas or Chashakas.

veral parts of a human body (see Adhyaya III, sloka 76).

Adhyaya II.

Sloka 1. in the notes to sloka 8 of Adhyaya I the
several parts of the Kalapurusha(@tegee )beginning with the
head were stated to be counted from Mesha andithose of the
owner of a horoscope from the Lagna. This is so far as
the external appearance, etc. of the several parts of the body
are concerned. Now the inherent qualities of the person
are to be ascertained from the planets. These planets are
then known as Karakas(®1zeq:)and appertain to the owner of
the horoscope just in the same way as they do to the
Kalapurusha.

he is alone, he is more a benefic than a malefic.

If there is a planet in the Lagna, then the season typified
by that planet should be predicted. When there are more
than one planet in the Lagna, then it is with reference to the
strongest planet. If there is no planet in the Lagna, it is only
in that case that the season should be predicted with refer-
ence to the planet owning the Lagna Drekkana.

lords are given in the following statement:—

Suppose at the time of a query, the Vrischika Navamsa in
the Mithuna lagna to have risen (2 signs 4 degrees 12 minu-
tes), and the position of Mars, the lurd of Vrischika, to be 9
signs 11 degrees 13 minutes. The period typified by the same
planet is day. According to the first view, Vrischika Navam-
sa being the 2nd in the Mithuna lagna, the completion or
success of the business hasto be predicted to happen in 2
days. According to the second view. as Mesha, the Navamsa
occupied by Mars, happens to be the 6th when counted from
Vrischika, the time in which the success or otherwise is
expected to occur will be 6 days.

qe, iv waaqs and v ygaw?] Vide Slokas 16% to 18 Ad-
hyaya IIL Sripatipadhati.

and (vii) ataqae.

Slokas 42-45 In order to a Sab the natural friendship or
otherwise of planets with respect to any one of the rest, the
following general rule is laid down by @eaqrara Viz.,

BEIRAMITAATANSEM Yau sasT saa |
asta fara at ara a aatea at aot: I

Planets are friendly to any given planet when they
happen to own the 5th, 12th, 2nd, 4th, 8th, and 9th houses
counted from the planet’s Moolatrikona Rasi, or when they
happen to own the planet’s exaltation Rasi. The rest are his
enemies.

Take for example the case of the Sun. His
Moolatrikona Rasi is faz. The 5th and 8th houses from faz
are We and Hta respectively, and both of ther are owned by
Jupiter. He is therefore a friend of the Sun. The. 12th
house from faz is H2% and the Moon, the lord of that sign;
is also his friend. The 4th and 9th houses from faz are TTF
and ag respectively and Mars is the lord of these two signs,
and is thus a friend of the Sun. The lord of the 2nd house
from faz is Mercury. As this planet is also the lord of an-—
other Rasi, v2z., faraa, which is the 1'th (from faz), a house
not mentioned in the sloka of MAAN quoted above, he must,
be considered as partly friendly and partly inimical, that
is, a neutral planet. ‘he Sun’s saqcitar is He and its lordis
Mars, who has already been proved to be his friend. Thus it
will be seen that Mars, Jupiter, and the Moon are the friends
of the Sun, and Mercury his neutral, The other two planets,
viz. Venus and Saturn are his enemies. Similarly in
the case of other planets. cf. Faia |

Besides the ten 3qeq: or conditions described in slokas
16-18 supra and the three ones mentioned in the present sloka,
there are some more eqeqt:; which have been ascribed to the
several planets due to their being placed in certain posi-
tions. An accurate knowledge of these appears to be very
essential for making correct predictions. It has often been
seen that, in the case of 2 or more persons born at one and
the same time (say in the same Lagna, in the same day, month
and year), the phalas or effects are not at all similar but quite
different. This is mostly due to the entire change in their
2yqqeat; in which the planets are situated in these horoscopes,

96

& 762
For instance, one ought to naturally expect @ratagte if a
benefic planet occupies the 5th house and Tara if a malefic
one should occupy the same. But itis stated that benefic
planets in the Ayr state in the slyeq and (agi sjqwI occupying
the 5th house do more harm than good for progeny, while
malefics in the same position and in the same conditions are
capable of helping the @zataafe of the native. As the

several qe: of the p.anets are thus seen to play a very

important part in moulding the nature of their effects during
any birth, a detailed description of the same here will not be
considered out of place. :

Six varieties have formerly been eared by Sambhu
regarding the conditions of planets, viz., (1) sia ashamed or
-abashed), (2) atfaaqq (haughty or conceited), (3) ata (hungry),
(4) aia (thirsty or greedy), /5) qiza Wiehe or Goes
and (6) atfa (agitated or excited).

(i) A planet is said to be ina ai@a condition when it
occupies the 5th house in conjunction with Rahu, Ketu, the
Sun, Saturn or Mars. 7

Git). It is<said to be Waa when it is in gi exaltation
position or in its Mulatrikona. | |

Gli} Sit isin a agpaa state when it occupies an inimi-
cal house oris in conjunction with Saturn or an inimical
planet or aspected by an inimical planet.

(iv) Ifthe planet being in a watery sign be at the same
time aspected by an inimical planet and unaspected by bene-
fic ones, it is said to be in a @ff@ state.

iv) But if it occupies a friend’s house and be in conjunc-
tion with a friendly planet or Jupiter or be aspected by
another friendly planet, it is in a wea condition. |

-(vi) Lastly, its condition will be atta when it is
eclipsed by the Sun and has on it the aspect of malefics « or
that of an inimical planet.

There are twelve other conditions of planets which have
also to be specially noticed. They in their order are

(1) ara (lying down,) (2) sqaaqr (sitting), (3) Raat
(with the hand on the eye), (4) g@raqra (making bright or
irradiating) (5) WAA=zBe idesire to go), (6) Waa (going”,
(7) q@urataata (dwelling or being in an assembly), (8) siaTA
(coming) (9)atswa (eating), 10) gatecar (desire to dance),
(11) ASE (delight, joy, pleasure) and (12) faat (sleep).

To find the particular sqrt of any planet, the following
is the method to be adopted:—

765

Find the product of the following three numbers:—-

(1) The number representing the order of the planet
(counted from the Sun} whose aq has tobe found. (2) the
number representing the order of the Nakshatra (counted from
Aswini) in which the planet is situated; and (3) the number
representing the actual degree in which the planet is at the
time under consideration. To this product must be added (1)
the number indicating the order of the person’s Weqaqara (2) the
-number representing the order of the HaawSA (counted from
aT) and (3) the number representing the ghatikas that have
elapsed since Sunrise till the time of birth. Divide the re-
sult by 12. The remainder will indicate the order of the
aqeat (counted from Z4y4) of the planet.

_ Again, multiply the remainder above obtained by itself. To
this add the number given in the marginally noted table
under the initial letter of the name of the person concern-
ed. Divide the result by 12. To the remainder add the Kshepa

Adhyaya III

Out of the 80 slokas in this Adhyaya, 46 have been taken
from Jawtlah, viz., Slokas 1-4, 7-15, 21-25, 38-40, 43-44, 46-47,
58, 60-79.

Slokas 1-2. The faatfa Rasis are aq, STH, AIF, faz,

Tere, first half of AA, ART ant and Ara.
For a faarfasta the following conditions are necessary:—

(1) The malefic planets must be strong.

783

(2) The benefic ones should be weak.

(3) One of the neutral planet Mercury or Saturn should
be in a Kendra position with respect to the Lagna or aspect
the Lagna. |

(4) The Dwadasamsa occupied by the Moon should be a

faatlaaar.

Suppose there is a birth when the Moon is on the 12th
degree of Dhanus. As each Dwadasamsa consists of 24
degrees and the first Dwadasamsa in that Rasi begins with
Dhanus, the Dwadasamsa occupied by the Moon is Mesha.
The birth of a goat or sheep has therefore to be predicted,
and so on.

Suppose the 14th degree of Mithuna tobe rising. Ther
the rising Navamsa is the fifth in facta, v.e., Kumbha.

785

which is only a land sign. mf at the time the position of
Saturn (the lord of -Kumbha) be. apa, the number of trees
will be the number of Rasis ‘separating ‘Saturn from the
Lagna 7.e. from faga to CIES EA i.e. 6 Again, from the slokas
of artadt quoted above, the following has also to be noted:
If the lord of the rising Navamsa either occupy his exaltation
sign or be retrograde in his motion, the number already ob-
tained should be trebled. If he be in his qufaq or his own
Navamsa or Rasi or Drekkana, such number should be doubl-
ed. For example, if Saturn in the instance cited be retrograde
in his motion, or. instead of occupying aan be in Get,
his exaltation zat, the: number 5 ( qe@t being the 5th from
fia) should be:trebled. We shall thus have a tay But if Saturn
should occupy the 4th Navamsa of qa, vig-WRL (his own
place), the number of trees willbe only 5x 2.or 10, and so on.

Menses appear in women every month when the Moon
occupies an Anupachaya Rasi (1st, 2nd, 4th, 5th, “th, 8th, 9th,
& 12th’, and is atthe same time aspected by Mars. The
Moon is water; Mars is fire. When water and fire combine,
the resultant is bile. When the bile mixes with the blood,
menses occur.

If, in the case of a woman as soon as she bathes after the
stoppage of the menses, the Moon in its orbit should occupy
an Upachaya Rasi and be at the same time aspected by
powerful Jupiter, she will have sexual intercourse with her
husband. If the Moon in the above said Upachaya position
be aspected by the Sun, she will have sexual union with an
officer of the King. If the aspecting planet be Mars, the
union will be with a voluptuary. If it be Mercury, the
intercourse will be with a fickle-minded person. If Venus be
the aspecting planet, it will be with a beautiful lover. If it
be Saturn, she will have criminal intimacy with an ordinary
servant. Ifthe Moon be aspected by several malefic planets
who are not occupying their own houses at the time, the
woman will become a courtezan. |

The above combinations need not necessarily be with

respect to the male or female. They may also be with res-
pect to the time of conception or Adhana Lagna (aratasa).

Another meaning can also be attributed to the sloka as

follows: |
FSA BSA: Tih: Taserat A wTEHz: |

So zff in the sloka means fasar (Pingala) and ee
means get. These are the winds passing through the right
and left nadis (att=—arteries and veins-). aantaterst will
mean semen and blood or the male and female energy. The
Sloka will then mean |

(i) If the author of conception has, at the time of connec-
tion, strong wind passing through the right aff, then the
female will conceive a male child and if in the left a female
child.

(ii) If semen be greater, the female will conceive a

male child and if blood, a female child.
In the case of equality of wind in the right and left nadis
or of the semen and blood, the female will conceive a eunuch

Sloka 16. In line 5 of the translation of this sloka, read
‘male’ for ‘female’. The last quarter of this sloka reads thus
in medtitt

TaIsy AY Was caiftaratie®.
But the reading in the text is the more generally accept-

ed one.
A few of-the objectionable periods are

(a) Nakshatras  yzoft, rae, Stet. ARTA, gararet
and qahateqat.

(b) syst and other Vishtiriktas. and

(c) Sankrama and such other sacred days, fasting days,

birth days, anniversary days and their previous ones. For
detailed information, see Muhurta Sastras.

TANITA HAIMA aya Mane Fe BRA.

Suppose the apqraaa or Geez to be 3 signs 8° 12,’ 20”
and the position of the Moon at the time 4 signs 10° 25’ 35’ As
the Moon is in the 5th Dwadasamsa of Simha, the Dwadasamsa
is Dhanus. The birth has to be predicted when the:Moon passes

through the 5th Rasi counted from Dhanus, z.e. Mesha. This is
the view of some.

According to others the birth should be _ predicted
thus:— Find by counting from Mesha the order of the
Rasi representing the Dwadasamsa; when the Moon traverses
through so many Rasis from the Dwadasamsa Rasi, birth
will happen. According to this view, Dhanus happens to be
the 9th from Mesha; the birth will happen when the Moon is
in the 9th Rasi from Dhanus. 7.e. Simha. The former view
seems more rational.

Then, to find the the exact position of the Moon, we have

2 of aq as having been traversed by the Moon at

birth time, or 5°116 or the second quarter of the star Aswini,

To know the time, we proceed thus:

The Lagna is 8° 12’ 20” in aH which is a night sign.
The exact time will therefore be at 30 ghatikas
(the period of night) or at 8 ghatikas, 1244 Vighatikas in the
night.

799

Balabhadra also adds that only in the case of the Moon
and the lord of the 5th bhava at the time of query or Aaa
ocupying one and the same Rasi, it has to be predicted that
the pregnant woman will be delivered of her child at that very
instant, and not otherwise. | :"

From the plural numbers used in the above two quotations,
it would appear that the term “malefics’’ need not necessarily
be confined to Saturn and Mars, but may be extended to weak
Moon, Rahu and Ketu. Butitis not so. And by “rraq” all
the five houses of malefics, viz a, faz, arm, WRT and ay
are not meant, but only a, faz and at. Here the difference
between the terms qTqaq and RTA may be noted.

806

The one means “all the houses owned by malefics” while
the other confines them to “their odd signs.” cf, FEAT

Samudrajataka do not mean “the 7th house” but mean
“eclipsed.’’ |

For visible and invisible portions of the zodiac at any time,

IGE says r
“awit oat Rs ae Sa aunt 1
aa wauradeaial ga searsqeaa Ii

The text gives the generally accepted rule for ascertain-
ing the actual number of persons present at the time of con-
finement. Some authorities view it otherwise, viz., that the
number of persons within the lying-in-chamber should be
guessed by the number of planets in the visible hemisphere,
while the number without should be reckoned by the number
of planets in the invisible portion of the zodiac. Cf. aitfaater.

SEMSMAAPAASATAEA TAH AEA: |

SWASE SALT USNWTA ASTSTASE II
Also sftaarat

SEAT: equrafanreTs |

seTEVaa searmaceax aa Il

This view is not accepted by Varahamihira and that is
why he said qzs-quqq in the sloka in the text.
But when the Lagna and the Moon arein one and the
same Kasi, the number will be as stated in alerat, v2.

In the light of the above quotations, RIT fanaa: would
appear to be the original and correct reading: and TANT
facia: must be an interpolation in Varahamihira’s work

and Juret. Varahamihira has used arsitaa:—O1 is another
which means more than two malefics, 2e., three

reading

823

or more. Rahu and Ketu are not reckoned as forming part of
the malefics in this yoga, for, at the end ofthe faAHEaTla,
Skanda has said.
ay aglaw: Ga Aan: |
aaa Aare Heasaesiewsy |
The three malefics can therefore be none other than the
Sun, Mars and Saturn.

ward dat a Aras: | That is, if the Moon in the 6th or the 8th
place be not aspected by any planet, the child will not die.

fic ones, then too, the child will not die. In support of the

The word AA in aayaated Wtq wer can mean only
Saturn in the present instance and no other malefic planet.
For an eclipse of the Moon can occur only on a full-moon day
and the Sun will then be in the 7th place or in direct opposi-
tion. Consequently, Mercury will not be near the Moon.

The first half of the sloka is also capable of being
translated thus: “When the Moon is eclipsed, irrespective

of its position, and the Lag na is occupied by a malefic planet
and Mars be in the 8th house, &c.”

In order that the four planets may be aspected by Jupiter,
the latter must occupy the 5th house from the Lagna. If he
should aspect only some of them, or be weak though he might
aspect all the four planets, the child will die. It will escape
death only in case Jupiter in full strength aspects all the four
planets. cf. Jacqrarqea

Sloka 39. The time taken by the Moon for making one
revolution is 27°32305 days approximately. The Moon there-
fore makes about 13 revolutions in the course of a year. When-
ever the Moon in the course of his orbit becomes powerful and
is aspected by strong malefic planets on reaching any one of
the three places referred to in the text, death will happen.

in a tabular form for easy reference. |

Explanation :—There are three sets of yogas under each
of ata, Weyq and SONYA. That particular kind of Ayus will
have to be predicted under which both the conditions which
constitute each set are satisfied. Thus, taking the first set
under each of the heads of Ayus, (i) if the lords of the Lagna
and the 8th house are both in moveable Rasis, draiza will
have to be predicted; (ii) if they occupy moveable and fixed
signs respectively, qeqaya will have to be stated; (iii) if
they are in @xz and gaz Rasis, then the native will be

weqrga. Similarly with reference to the remaining two sets.

a particular limit from the Sun, his light being then over-
powered by that of the Sun. This limit in the case of the
several planets is as follows:

The Moon when within 12° from the Sun.
Mars when within 17"
Mercury when within 14°
but when retrograde 12°

When there are two planets one malefic and the other
benefic occupying the Lagna, the planet that is nearer to the
rising Navamsa is alone to be considered and not the. others.
If the malefic planet be nearer, then the reduction enjoined
has to be made; but if the benefic be nearer, then no reduction
need be made. This is the view of the commentator.

Some are of opinion that the number of years of reduction
is obtained by multiplying the total number of years already
obtained by the number of Navamsas between wq and the

Navamasa lagna. This view is not favoured either by the
commentator or by Kalyanavarman, the author of Saravali.

The word Tt in the sloka does not include the waning
Moon, for, says the commentator: qa PIISeAT BUBesA A

The Sun in the 7th place suffers a reduction of th of his
Ayurdaya period ‘sloka9). He therefore gives 17°790 minus
2°965 or 14°825 years.

The Moon being in an inimical house suffers a reduction
by 4rd. (Sloka-7). He therefore gives 22°263 minus 7°421 or
14°842 years. There is no reduction under sloka 9, as he does
not occupy any one of the latter 6 houses.

Mars occupies the 9th house and therefore loses Uth of
its ayus. (sloka 9). He therefore gives 7°665 minus 1'916 or
4°749 years.

Mercury is not eclipsed by the Sun being more than 14
degrees apart. Nor is hein any one of the latter 6 houses,
He does not therefore suffer any reduction. ,

Jupiter occupies the 12th house. As he is a benefic pla-
net, he loses only half (instead of the whole) of his ayus (sloka
9). He therefore gives 5°193 years. He is occupying an inimical
house and has therefore to suffer a further reduction by 14rd.
But as he is retrograde in motion at the time heis exempt
from this (sloka 7).

Venus being in the 8th house has to lose half of 1/5th
(as he is benefic) of his ayus. He therefore gives 16°259 minus
1°626 or 14°633 years.

Saturn does not suffer any reduction under sloka 9, as he
is in the 6th house. Though he is within 15 degress from
the Sun and consequently eclipsed by the Sun’s rays, still, he
has no Astangata reduction (see sloka-7).

Sloka 22: «The WAATS system founded by Mahendra
has been in vogue from a very long time and has been recog-
nised by eminent astrologers such as Manittha, Maya, Yavana
and Badarayana. A brief description of the method of obtain-
ing the correct number of Rasmi or rays of planets is given j
below : :

The seven planets from.the Sun onwards have respective-
ly 10, 9, 5,5, 7, 8 and 5 rays when they are in their full exal-
tation position, the corresponding number of rays when in.
their depression being zero in each case. The number of rays
which a planet in any intermediate position is entitled to has
therefore to be determined by mere rule-of-three.

When a planet has emerged from its aft or depression
and is on its way towards its exaltation, its rays are said to be
MAGA or facing towards the front. These rays are declar-
ed to be capable of producing benefic results to the native in
avery short time. The reverse is the case with the qsya
or down-looking rays of planets which have fallen from their
Zay or exaltation and are going towards their depression.

The rays have to undergo some multiplications and
reductions, before their utilisation for purposes of prediction.

When a planet occupies a Dwadasamsa which is his own,
his exaltation, or that of a friendly planet, his rays are to be
doubled. The same remark applies when the planet is retro-
gerade in his motion or is in his AT Rasi. But if the Dwada-
samsa be that of an enemy or the planet’s atazear, the rays
suffer a reduction by eth portion. All planets —Venus and
Saturn excepted lose their rays when they become 3itqita
or eclipsed. When the retrograde motion of a planet is about
to cease, the rays suffer a reduction by lth.

849

The quality of a horoscope varies directly with the tota]
number of rays given by the planets at the time of birth. Per-
sons with 1 to5 rays will be very insignificant, miserable,
low-minded, unlucky, etc; those born with 6 to 10 rays will be
dependants with no permanent home and poor ; those with 10
to 15 rays will be somewhat better, being virtuous, of good
temper, good specimens of their family &c.; those with 15 to
20 rays will in addition be rich, famous and respected by their
relations, and so on.

It is also stated that the native should be declared long-
lived when the number of rays exceed 25, as one of medium
life when itis between 15 and 20, and short-lived when the
number falls short of 15.

several chapters on the subject in Brihat Parasarahora,
Saravali, &c.

Sloka 13. That is, if the 22nd Drekkana is a fiery one
(vide Adhyaya IX-sloka 112, infra) and is owned by a malefic
planet, the body will be burnt; if it be a watery one or benefic

868

(vide Adhyaya IX, slokas 113-114, infra), the body will be
thrown into the water. If the Drekkana in question being a
mixed one (vide Adhyaya IX, sloka 115) is benefic but occupied
by a malefic planet, or malefic but occupied by a benefic pla-
net, then the body is neither burnt nor thrown in water, but
is allowed to dry in the ordinary course (2.e. by expo ure to.

the Sun and wind). »

Sloka 48. This and the next two slokas as well as sloka
02 are from g@qlam. In the translation, for the words “two
legs and two hands,” read “four legs and four hands.”

First half. 'The above result should not be predicted un-
less the characteristic combinations mentioned are complete.
if the characteristics are incomplete, an inordinate largeness
of the organs named should be foretold. Some commentators
take the word fate in the sloka to mean WaTRIN of Mer-
cury, that is eq. But this has to be rejected on the autho-
rity of aTIfaT who distinctly says:

aediaue: Gaga TH |
faanfsicteett AaAAHISTEAAT Il

Second half. By the words “malefic planets are in the
Riksha Sandhis,” it is to be understood that all the three male-
fics, viz., Saturn, Mars, and the Sun are jointly or severally to
be in the last portion of any one ate more of Harm, aT and
ita. By the words “after a long time,’’ nothing more is
meant than“later than the ordinary time within which a child
may be naturally expected to- speak.” .

The last quarter of the sloka, viz. AMPA SA fir fara
leads to the inference that such Moon should also have male-
fic aspect to make the person mute.

873

This is supported by aTIfaT who. says
gatasarreae: Wrsrz saa |
aR: Wasa: tear wat Fra
farenaen aa ae ae AT

Unless therefore all the three malefics are thus placed
and the Moon has a malefic aspect, the prediction should not
be made. If there be only two malefics thus placed, then
frugality or smallness of speech and if only one, wavering
slow or stammering speech should be foretold. -But if such
Moon has the aspect of one or more benefic planets, the
person will begin to speak later than when he would other-
wise be naturally expected to speak It must thus be remem-
bered that the aspect of malefics is also required to make the
native mute, and the aspect of benefics to counteract it.

Sloka 51. This slokais from waj@tzj where the reading
is slightly different; viz., Bg SRR att: &c. In order to
complete the idea given in sloka50 above, this has to be read
with the following first half of the next sloka in that work,

Ul, -<
fag vanera afe aheractira: |.

Its omission from the text must only be accidental. The ©
¢ranslation should therefore be thus:

Mars occuping the first (rising) Drekkana of the Lagna
and aspected by the Sun, the Moon and Saturn, produces a
headless child. The child will be born armless if Mars
occupy the first (rising) Drekkana of the 5th house and be
aspected by the same three planets. The child born will have
no legs if Mars under the same aspect occupy the first Drek-
kana of the 9th house from the Lagna.

be occupied by the Sun alone and be aspected by Mars and
Saturn, the child will be blind of the right eye. He will be
blind of the left eye if the Moon alone should occupy faz and
be aspected by Mars and Saturn.

Sloka 3. The first sentence of the translation should read
thus: “If all the planets are associated with the Moon’s hora
(=eqetzt), the person born will be a famous King.”

In the last line of the translation, for “9th,” read “10th.”

Sloka 4. In the 2nd line of the translation of this sloka,
for the words “the 10th and the 7th bhavas,” read “the 10th,
the 7th and the 4th bhavas.” The number of yogas satisfying
all the conditions described in this sloka will, if carefully
analysed, be found to be very limited.

The following horoscope is suggested as an example for
this sloka.
| Meena Lagna; the Sun in Simha; the Moon in Meena.
Mars in Vrischika; Mercury in Kanya; Jupiter in Dhanus;
Venus in Tula; and Saturn in Makara.

But see the following sloka from @TrTadt.

qaagaam: sar aaa: qucasiseanaeM: |
af. aala ndtaia: saat aaacfanet- aa adtqata: |

Sloka 6. According to FTeAsTs, TEstdth, Saya
and other works, four or more planets (other than the Moon)
aspecting a Vargottama Lagna or Moon will make the person
born at that time a King.

cf. FASNAH
aaa GH AR aT aeratsa: |
aquaseee aa gia: Brat: Il

Also @izradt |
TAA SuaaiaHical AHA Wasa |
aquesraatsiaeaar Fitter: MUP TATRN 37: Il

Also FlaRazLT
aiaasmaat ae a ht Gees a aquishesde: |

onus na aaa a anfaa
agunaaiae: Wane at AAS |

But our author has gone further by restricting the post-
tion of the planets. According to slokas 30-31 of the 2nd
Adhyaya, Saturn in the 4th or the 11th house, Mars in the 6th
-or the 10th house and Jupiter in the 5th.or the 9th house, all
counted from the Lagna or the Moon, are also capable of
aspecting either of them respectively as all planets are while
they are in the 7th house. Our author has rejected the aspects
of Saturn from the-llth house, of Mars from the 6th house and
of Jupiter from the 5th’ and 9th houses. He only accepts
the aspects of Saturn from the 4th house, of Mars from the
10 house and of Jupiter and other planets from the 7th house.
His object would appear to be to place all the planets in the —
three Kendras, 4th, 7th and 10th and in such a manner as to
be capable of bestowing their full aspect on the Lagna or the
Moon as the case may be. . The least number of :planets thus

111

882

situated and capable of aspecting the Vargottama Lagna or
the Moon is four. sextape: i.e., by planets other than the
Moon” has reference only so far as Lagna yogas are con-
cerned, for the Moon cannot aspect itself. But the question
is “Will the aspect of the Moon on the Lagna nullify the
yoga if it is otherwise complete ?” The answer is in the
negative. In this case, the Moon’s aspect does not count, being
productive of neither good nor evil and the Moon cannot be
one of the planets making up the least number of four above

stated. cf. Jeammge.

or four planets in their exaltation ( TAT), in their own houses

the 2nd bhava. The text lends itself also to the above inter-

Venus together with the lord of the Lagna, who is also strong,
is in the 2nd wre whichis neither the planet’s , Venus’) de-
pression sign, nor is owned by an enemy, the person born will
become a King.”

1 Each one occupying the other’s house or Kendra
or Kona.

2 Both planets aspecting each other.
3 Any one of the two planets aspecting the other.
4 Both planets.occupying the same house or Varga, etc.

From the above, it is also clear that benefic planets in
exaltation make the king virtuous and that if the planets in
exaltation which go to raise him to kingship be toth male-
fic and benefic, he will be of a mixed nature.

But Wtaarat says that malefics in exaltation do not

bestow royalty on the person born under their auspices; he
will be wealthy and at the same time cruel, angry and
quarrelsome. cf.

qaeatasttar a water Sat at: |
Peg Rrenaaret eq: arPra: aaefhar: I

But arratarex who agrees with the majority has laid
down that planets in exaltation do not deprive the person of
royalty for the simple reason of their being malefics. If
they have the power of conferring royalty on the person
they will certainly do so, but he will be ill-natured because
they who helped him to the ec ats are malefics. cf. JENNA.

Sloka 26. In the third Qe “MrqaaaTaay” appears to
be a better reading, and the translation would then be “and

Saturn is in Apr.”

Three Rajayogas are mentioned by qrtetatez and others
as described below:

(1) The Moon and Jupiter in sgq@, Mars in WRT and
Venus in Ata identical with the Lagna.

(2) The Moon and Jupiter in gq, Mars in ART and
Mercury in #eyQTt identical with the Lagna.

895

(3) The Moon, Jupiter and Venus in qa, Mars and
Saturn in Wax and Mercury in Hrqr identical with the
Lagna.

Of the above three yogas, the third yoga is the best and
our author would appear to have described it as the sloka
is capable of that interpretation. The translation will then
be “When the Moon in conjunction with Jupiter and Venus
occupies agZa, Mercury iS in Heqt identical with the
Lagna and Mars and Saturn are in AR, &c.” cf. TESTA.

The translation of the first half should read thus: “A

carefully noted.

Sloka 57. Gargaas already pointed out, disagrees from
the author of this sloka. He says that:Saturn in da, ada

or ta identical with the Lagna is capable of- producing
kingship, and in any other sign he will cut short the period
- of life-(vide the sloka quoted in page 759 under notes to
sloka 67 of Adhyaya II). As for ay, it is Saturn’s depres:
sion sign and as such he is weak. It is therefore contrary
to the principles already stated and to common sense that
Saturn in WY identical with the rising sign will bestow

kingship and has to be rejected in the absence of other
authorities or other yogas.

There are 31 varieties of OAT, 31 of aq and 180 of

Take for example the TARA. The second house
from the Moon may contain one, two, three, four or all the

five planets (viz- Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus & Saturn).
5 5 Pee we A,
This will be in 5, 10, 10, 5 and 1 or 31 ways- IC HO POSE.

Similarly with the sTqqTart.

Now take the case of 2 aq yoga where the second
house from the Moon is Occupied by only one planet. In
order to make this a Dhurudhura aia, the 12th house from
the Moon may contain one, two, three or four planets. This

Get, ee
can be in 4+6+4 pe C+C] or 15 ways. So for one
| ‘Bei
- @aw yoga where the 2nd house is occupied by one planet
there are 15 varieties of Dhurudhura ay possible. Therefore,
for the 5 varieties of WAR with one planet in the 2nd
house there are 5x15 or 75 varieties of Dhurudhura aya.

Next, in the case of a Waqraqty with 2 planets in the 2nd

house, to make this a Dhurudhura yoga, the 12th house
may contain oue, two or three planets. This can bein

a a
5 OS vas newb or ( ways. Therefore for the 10 such

varieties o GATT with two planets in the 2nd house, 10 x 7

908

or 70 varieties of Dhurudhura yogas are possible. Similarly
in the case of a BaHalT with 3 planets in the 2nd house in
order to convert it into a Dhurudhura yoga, the 12th house
may contain one or two planets. This will be in 2+1or3
ways. For the 10 such varieties of GARANT with 3 planets in
the 2nd house, we have i0 x3 or 30 varieties of Dhurudhura.

In the same way, for the 5 varieties of Gama with 4
planets in the 2nd house we have 5 varieties of Dhurudhura.

In the case of the @aFr yoga which has all the 5
planets in the 2nd house, no Dhurudhura yoga is possible.

The total number of varieties of Dhurudhura yoga is thus
79 +70 + 30 + 5=180.

Slokas 86-105. Varahamihira does not differentiate the
effect of the planets by their positions in the 2nd or in the
12th house so far as these yogas are concerned. He at-
tributes the effects to the planets causing the yoga without
reference to their being in the 2nd or the 12th house.

Sloka 107. With regard to the Moon’s capacity of doing
good or evil with reference to these three yogas, Varaha-
mihira says.
AVIHS Use sea hansadagay BASHA SeagTaAT Il
That is, for persons born in the day time, the Moon in
any of the first 6 houses is auspicious and in any of the

other 6 houses is inauspicious. The reverse effect is to be
understood in the case of persons born in the night time.

theg and waa and others also say so with reference
to these yogas. These therefore deserve consideration.

devoid of its Lad effects if Jupiter, while being in the 6th or
the 8th house from the Moon, is in a Kendra from the Lagna.

Sloka 118 In the 2nd quarter of this sloka some books
read afer rea ataresen. The translation will then
be “wherein takes place the birth of a leader of an army or
a king’s minister or a king.”

The benefic planets referred to are Mercury, Jupiter
and Venus. :

The commentator of this sloka says that the interpreta-
tion put by some that all the 3 places, wiz. 6th, 7th & 8th
houses from the Moon should be occupied each by one of
the three benefics Mercury Jupiter and Venus to constitute
an STP TTT is not correct, and quotes in support thereof the

following from sTaatta who recognises 7 sorts of sIfsyaqtv.

Praia Ta TS aeTeTATAIST TAHA |
aa: @ weal TeRat Ga Get Il
These seven sorts are caused according as the three
benefic planets occupy one or more of the three houses, 6th,
7th and 8th places from the Moon. That is, they might
occupy (1) all the 3 houses, 6th, 7th and 8th; (2) the 6th and
’th; (3) the 6th and 8th; (4) the 7th and 8th; (5) the 6th
only; (6) the 7th only; (7) the 8th only.
He also adds
ATARATSAMATCaT Alea: TALSTAANT: SAT |
amg: Gates fetfereaas: Il

Badarayana is of opinion that according as all the
benefic planets happen to possess superior, medium or
ordinary strength, a person born under the yoga will become
a king, a minister or a commander. cf.

Sloka 167. Varahamihira has explained:in this sloka
the reason why some ofthe astrologers have omitted to
treat in their works seperately of the 3 sieTq and the 2 2@
yogas.

From the statement (printed in the next two pages) show-

ing the several divisions of the Nabhasa yogas, it will be seen
- that the 3 ajs7q and the 2@@ yogas sometimes (but not al-
ways) happen to be identical with some of the ata and
the aT yogas.

For instance, suppose all the planets in a certain horoscope
happen to be in Ay and Hay. As these two signs are moveable
ones, the yoga caused is Taf and will be identical with wert
if the Lagna happens to be either HT or PzH. But if any
other Tifst (other than Wy or Ho ) be the Lagna, and all the
planets are as described in the above, there is no WT yoga
though it may still be called CS.

Again, if all the planets be in Hy and get and if one of
these Rasis be the Lagna, then too the yuga is t@q since both

the signs are moveable ones. As Wy and qe@t happen to be

the Ist and 7th houses and all the planets are said to be in
these two houses, the yoga ig aT.

Suppose all the planets to occupy #rgq and Hta, two of
the dual signs, The yogais a@. If one of these two Rasis
be also the Lagna, the yoga is known both as aAx and aa.
But if faua or Seger be the Lagna, it will be both qe and
az. If any other Rasi be the Lagna, it will only be a a@ata.
Examples like these can be multiplied.

The question now arises as to why then Varahamihira
‘makes special mention of these (211374 and @#) yogas in his
work. The answer is, all the possible instances of these yogas
are not included in the atta and xa yogas. The 2pyqa
and @# yogas may be these and may not be these. It is
therefore that the author has deemed it fit to treat them
separately.

Again, it is said of the two @@ yogas that other authors
have described the effects of benefic and malefic planets oc-
cupying the Kendras and as these are also the effects of the
two @% yogas, they have omitted to treat of them separately.
Varahamihira treats of these separately in order to make it
known that the two are @lwyaq yogas and as such their effects

are felt throughout life, and not like the other yogas whose
effects are felt only during their Dasa or Antardasa period

and not afterwards. cf.

There are thus 4 varieties of 72T yoga, viz: All the planets

may occupy (1) Ist and 4th houses (2) 4th and 7th houses.
(3) 7th and 10th houses (4) 10th and Ist houses.
. The Yavanas recognise these as 4 distinct yogas and

call them respectively as Tet, 3g, faz and Sad.

Varahamihira has another verse after this sloka in which
he states that he has simply described the as and other
yogas (that is gq and the yoga mentioned in gI- Ch. XI-20)
adopting the view of the former writers, such as wa, Jaq and
others, meaning thereby that he personally does not recog-
nise them; for, he asks “How can Mercury and Venus occupy
the 4th house from the Sun?” This sloka which has been
left out by Vaidyanatha-for reasons best known to himself
is reproduced below:

qiarengaivn AA AWA: Hal: |
aga waa gaisateat wad: F277 I

The same doubt is expressed by WNT, a later writer,
for he says:

Afarottarata Bena waleat asaat fe ant |
qaisay aaa faaat at aaanfata qatar |

But readers who are familiar with the working of the Bha-
vasphuta process (aTaTEHT) described in detail in shafaqsta,
Adhyaya I and in the notes thereto will easily see that it is
not impossible as we go to higher latitudes to have some of
_ the bhavas uncommonly short and others extraordinarily
long, so.that Mercury and Venus may happen to be in the
4th wraq with respect to the Sun, though not in the 4th TTSt
‘as interpreted by Varahamihira.

It will therefore be seeen that Wa, Wea, WT and others
have not erred in treating of these yogas as possible ones, if
the yogas in question are meant to refer to the positions of

the ages in the SICA S and not to the TifaTam.

The two Dala yogas will cuincide neither with the 2ysTzy
yogas nor with the aft yogas. They might coincide with —
RAT yogas~ qrury, aitaet, GTal or HztI— in which case the

_ Again, it has already been stated (Vide slokas from Jeatl-
AG, Aleast and yorRT quoted above) that where the apsyzy
yogas coincide with other yogas, they cease to be 3I1s7q yogas.
So that the «zqr and sta yogas become null and void
(eclipsed or inoperative) when they coincide with any of the
atta yogas. But suppose the amy and @eaT yogas to
coincide with each other. How are they to be then treated ?
According to the commentator, if the coincidence relates to
the PAT, 4} and At yogas, the yogas are to ‘be treated as
asta yogas only, but if it refer to the atw yoga, it is to be
treated as such and not as 3Ify@q orie-——otherwise there will be
no scope for the mes yoga at all.
